@@136693141 <h> Legal or illegal , Ireland 's emissions failure will damage the world <p> Approximately 40 additional data centres have been given the green light since the amended climate law was enacted . File photograph : Getty <p> In 2021 when Ireland 's amended climate law was going through the legislative process , critics noted it contained no legal mechanism to force compliance by government . But these concerns were dismissed in the hope the obligations on public bodies and government would steer the economy and society on to a net-zero emissions pathway . <p> If the law is working , it should align our investment and planning decisions with commitments under the Paris Agreement . This necessarily requires a long-term view , since emissions released today will be hanging around for a very long time . On human timescales and with currently available technology , carbon dioxide that we release into the atmosphere will stay there causing climate change to worsen for thousands of years . The more we put up there , the worse the problem gets . <p> The uncompromising physics of climate change simply ca n't @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ or by relying on unproven or immature technologies , or by promising to do good deeds elsewhere . <p> We 're all a little bit responsible -- this is true -- but big emitters have a particular duty to act , as do countries . Climate cases taken by citizens around the world have sought to clarify and enforce this duty , including the Irish " climate case " taken by Friends of the Irish Environment which was ultimately successful in the Supreme Court in 2020 . In 2024 in the KlimaSeniorinnencase , the European Court of Human Rights established a direct legal link between a state 's failure to meet carbon reduction targets and the violation of individual human rights . The Senior Women for Climate Protection had formed in 2016 to challenge the Swiss government 's failure to act effectively on climate change . <p> With so much at stake , not least mounting concerns over food and energy security , you 'd think our Government would be planning for a future without fossil fuels . However , legally binding limits are not being translated into practical policies @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ . It is deeply concerning that since the 2021 Climate Law was adopted , numerous planning approvals will substantially increase Ireland 's greenhouse gas ( GHG ) emissions or lock-in fossil fuel demand even further . For example , approximately 40 additional data centres have received approval since the amended climate law was enacted . <p> In March this year , the High Courtrefused an application by environmentalists to quash a decision of An Coimisi ? n Plean ? la that would allow the construction of a ? 1.6 billion , 200MW data centre campus in Ennis , Co Clare . According to Colin Doyle , a physicist who took the legal challenge along with Friends of the Irish Environment , the proposed data centre will emit 700,000 tonnes of greenhouse gases -- more carbon than 50,000 people would produce -- every year . This one data centre will consume 21 per cent of the carbon budget for the electricity sector by 2030 . <p> Yet the numbers in the case -- the additional emissions the data centre 's energy demand would generate -- were not deemed to be a @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ Court was satisfied by the argument made by the developer and An Coimisi ? n Plean ? la that , when considered in global or European terms , " the GHGemissions associated with the project are small " and that a commitment to procure renewable electricity through a Corporate Power Purchase Agreement would count as adequate mitigation in " net " terms . <p> In another recent High Court decision , Judge Richard Humphreys refused an application to quash permission for a 600MW power stationon the Shannon estuary , accepting the developer 's argument that the facility would replace existing , less efficient , and more carbon-intensive power generation . However , this ignores the cumulative impact of carbon dioxide and its residency in the atmosphere . Any additional emissions will cause additional climate harm : that 's the basic physical and moral challenge of climate change . <p> The fact that the atmosphere can not distinguish between legal and illegal emissions is apparently beside the point : the law is only interested in legal arguments , not mathematical ones . <p> In the Ennis case , Humphreys acknowledged that @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ scientifically questionable claim by the developer that procuring renewable energy in the future would compensate for the energy demand of the data centre . The court has essentially accepted the moral logic of " offsetting " , just as the medieval church took payments for the remission of sins known as " indulgences " ( a practice which ultimately triggered the Reformation ) . While the church could promise a reduction in temporal punishment in purgatory , the emissions from these facilities will stay in the atmosphere forever doing irreparable harm . <p> The legal situation following both the Ennis and Shannon LNG cases is one where it seems the courts will not act to prevent committed emissions from a combined demand of 600MW of additional and avoidable power-related emissions . This is deeply regrettable . 
@@136693194 <p> Under the new rules , data centres will be connected if they supply as much electricity to the network as they take out of it by building -- or contracting energy companies to build -- power generation plants on site or nearby . <p> For the first six years , that extra electricity can be generated with fossil fuels . After that , 80 per cent of the electricity will have to come from renewables such as wind or solar farms the data centres build or contract energy companies to build . These can be anywhere in the country . But that still allows 20 per cent of a very large volume of electricity to be generated from gas or oil . <p> Data centres use 22 per cent of all electricity in the country and that figure is projected to grow to more than 30 per cent by 2031 . <p> Tony Lowes of Friends of the Irish Environment welcomed the High Court 's decision to grant leave to challenge the arrangement . <p> " If it goes ahead as is , this policy would n't just open @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ expensive fossil gas -- it would swing it right off its hinges and lock Ireland into decades of greenhouse gas emissions at the very moment we need to reduce them , " he said . <p> Deirdre Duffy of Friends of the Earth said Ireland 's energy policy was " going in the wrong direction " . <p> " This case is seeking to press pause on that . Instead of cutting fossil fuels , which are driving the climate change Irish people are experiencing with floods and storms , we 're locking in more gas just to keep up with soaring demand from data centres . " <p> ClientEarth lead lawyer Natascha Hospedales said : " Countries across Europe are grappling with the energy demands of rapidly expanding data centres in the global race to scale AI . <p> " That 's exactly why regulators like the CRU must ensure their approach to the infrastructure boom is responsible and aligns with legally binding climate commitments . " <p> The CRU said it would not be appropriate to comment while the proceedings were ongoing .

In addition to conserving marine species and habitats , MPAs can support maritime economic activity and reduce the effects of climate change and ocean acidification . <p> MPAs can be found across a range of marine habitats , from the open ocean to coastal areas , intertidal zones , bays and estuaries . Marine protected areas are defined areas where human activities are managed to protect important natural or cultural resources . <p> The world 's first MPA is said to have been the Fort Jefferson National Monument in Florida , North America , which covered 18,850 hectares of sea and 35 hectares of coastal land @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ main drive for MPAs came much later . The current global movement can be traced to the first World Congress on National Parks in 1962 , and initiation in 1976 of a process to deliver exclusive rights to sovereign states over waters up to 200 nautical miles out then began to provide new focus <p> The Rio ' Earth Summit ' on climate change in 1992 saw a global MPA area target of 10% by the 2010 deadline . When this was not met , an " Aichi target 11 " was set requiring 10% coverage by 2020 . There has been repeated efforts since then to tighten up MPA requirements . <p> Marae Moana is a multiple-use marine protected area created on July 13th 2017 by the government of the Cook islands in the south Pacific , north- east of New Zealand . The area extends across over 1.9 million square kilometres . However , In September 2019 , Jacqueline Evans , a prominent marine biologist and Goldman environmental award winner who was openly critical of the government 's plans for seabed mining , was replaced as director of @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ . The move attracted local media criticism , as Evans was responsible for developing the Marae Moana policy and the Marae Moana Act , She had worked on raising funding for the park , expanding policy and regulations and developing a plan that designates permitted areas for industrial activities . <p> Criteria for identifying and selecting MPAs depends on the overall objective or direction of the programme identified by the coastal state . For example , if the objective is to safeguard ecological habitats , the criteria will emphasise habitat diversity and the unique nature of the particular area . <p> Permanence of MPAs can vary internationally . Some are established under legislative action or under a different regulatory mechanism to exist permanently into the future . Others are intended to last only a few months or years . <p> Yes , Ireland has MPA cover in about 2.13 per cent of our waters . Although much of Ireland 's marine environment is regarded as in " generally good condition " , according to an expert group report for Government published in January 2021 , it says that biodiversity loss @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ increasing pressures such as overexploitation , habitat loss , pollution , and climate change " . <p> The Government has set a target of 30 per cent MPA coverage by 2030 , and moves are already being made in that direction . However , environmentalists are dubious , pointing out that a previous target of ten per cent by 2020 was not met . <p> Conservation and sustainable management of the marine environment has been mandated by a number of international agreements and legal obligations , as an expert group report to government has pointed out . There are specific requirements for area-based protection in the EU Marine Strategy Framework Directive ( MSFD ) , the OSPAR Convention , the UN Convention on Biological Diversity and the UN Sustainable Development Goals . <p> Yes , the Marine Strategy Framework directive ( 2008/56/EC ) required member states to put measures in place to achieve or maintain good environmental status in their waters by 2020 . Under the directive a coherent and representative network of MPAs had to be created by 2016 . <p> Ireland was about halfway up the EU table @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ in a comparison published by the European Commission in 2009 . However , the Fair Seas campaign , an environmental coalition formed in 2022 , points out that Ireland is " lagging behind " even our closest neighbours , such as Scotland which has 37 per cent . The Fair Seas campaign wants at least 10 per cent of Irish waters to be designated as " fully protected " by 2025 , and " at least " 30 per cent by 2030 . <p> Nearly a quarter of Britain 's territorial waters are covered by MPAs , set up to protect vital ecosystems and species . However , a conservation NGO , Oceana , said that analysis of fishing vessel tracking data published in The Guardian in October 2020 found that more than 97% of British MPAs created to safeguard ocean habitats , are being dredged and bottom trawled . <p> Current protection in marine areas beyond 12 nautical miles is limited to measures taken under the EU Birds and Habitats Directives or the OSPAR Convention . This means that habitats and species that are not listed in the EU @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ internationally important , can not currently be afforded the necessary protection <p> Yes . In late March 2022 , Minister for Housing Darragh O'Brien said that the Government had begun developing " stand-alone legislation " to enable identification , designation and management of MPAs to meet Ireland 's national and international commitments . <p> The Government 's expert group report noted that MPA designations are likely to have the greatest influence on the " capture fisheries , marine tourism and aquaculture sectors " . It said research suggests that the net impacts on fisheries could ultimately be either positive or negative and will depend on the type of fishery involved and a wide array of other factors . <p> The same report noted that marine tourism and recreation sector can substantially benefit from MPA designation . However , it said that the " magnitude of the benefits " will depend to a large extent on the location of the MPA sites within the network and the management measures put in place .

@@136710544 <h> Norma at the Irish National Opera : " This is possibly going to be one of INO 's best shows ever " <p> Few operas carry the weight of expectation that Bellini 's Norma does . Since its premiere at the iconic La Scala in Milan in 1831 , it has stood at the pinnacle of the bel canto tradition , thanks to its musical beauty and captivating title role of a Druidic high priestess torn between love , duty and sacrifice , which demands a voice of almost supernatural scale . <p> This May , Irish National Opera are bringing their own production to Irish audiences , led by Kilkenny native Orpha Phelan . After moving to London and studying arts criticism , she fell in love with opera 's power to capture the full breadth of emotive storytelling . Fast forward and she 's an award-winning director who works at the highest level of the form . <p> And she 's relishing the challenge to put her own spin on Norma . <p> " Norma is on a pedestal , " she says . " Any opera @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ looks like . It 's going to be a challenge to direct something often regarded as the pinnacle of the whole bel canto genre , so I 'm looking forward to doing something quite unique with it . " <p> That uniqueness begins with the setting . The original is set in ancient Gaul , a world which Phelan notes there is little art or reference to draw from , leading her to transplant the action to a post-apocalyptic near-future . <p> " Rather than going way into the past into a place that we do n't really understand or identify with , there is a place just around the corner that we 're constantly being told we 're on the verge of , " she explains . " Let 's just say that we are bombarded with information about how the world is going wrong . Our news and our current affairs programmes are full of leaders making decisions which put our lives in jeopardy . We 've got devastating climate change and we 've got AI , which some of us are frightened into thinking will be the @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ amongst all of this despair , we find the need to cling to some hope in some way , shape or form . When we think of a world in a desperate place , we can understand why a spiritual leader like Norma would have an appeal , and how her faith would keep people going . " <p> Also central to Phelan 's vision is a feminist reclamation of the title character . In her eyes , the character of Norma has too often been staged as hysterical and irrational , a tendency that could be traced back to the fact that the librettist , composer and author of the source play were all men . <p> " There was a sense at the time of , ' Women are like this and they behave like that , ' " Phelan says . " It 's really important to me that we present Norma as a rational , thinking person . I prefer the idea of her sacrificing herself , not because she 's been betrayed by a man , but because she understands her death is necessary to @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ is the best thing for her family and her people . <p> " It 's fair to say this production will be drawing on powerful women throughout history . Like Cleopatra , who was known for her intelligence and diplomacy and political acumen . She 's a symbol of feminine power . Then I was looking at Boudica , who battled the powerful Roman Empire . I looked at Joan of Arc and even Grace O'Malley . " <p> Bringing this vision to life is a cast of real quality . Georgian soprano Salome Jicia takes the title role , Australian soprano Siobhan Stagg sings Adalgisa -- the younger priestess whose bond with Norma forms the emotional spine of the opera -- and tenor Mario Chang completes the triangle as their mutual lover Pollione . <p> In the pit meanwhile , is Maurizio Benini , a critically acclaimed conductor whose CV boasts productions at some of the world 's leading opera houses . <p> " To have somebody like that come to work with Irish National Opera puts it on another sort of scale , " Phelan says . " @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ , Covent Garden and La Scala . Certainly our singers are similar . So this is possibly going to be one of INO 's best shows ever . " <p> * Irish National Opera 's Norma runs from May 24 -- June 6 , with dates in Dublin , Wexford , and Cork . Tickets are on sale now . For more information visit irishnationalopera.ie.

@@136727446 <p> This experiment is " a clear sign of the effectiveness of SAF towards climate-compatible aviation " . Airbus <p> 0:00 / 0:00 <p> Jet fuel combustion produces not just CO2 but also soot particles that serve as the nuclei for contrail clouds -- thin cirrus clouds that capture solar radiation . Estimates put the global warming effect of contrail cirrus at around 57 mW/m ? ( compared to 34 mW/m ? for all of the accumulated CO2 ) worldwide . In other words , non-CO2 contributions ( contrails , aerosols , NO ? ) dictate the climate change contribution of aviation . <p> While airborne , water droplets freeze on these soot particles to form ice crystals , which remain in the atmosphere as cirrus clouds for several hours . However , hydroprocessed esters and fatty acids ( HEFA ) SAF , which is made from waste oils , contains negligible aromatic content and hence emits minimal soot particles and ice nuclei . Essentially , switching from kerosene to SAF can help reduce two sources of global warming -- contrails and CO2 -- simultaneously . <h> Case study : @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ ECLIF3 team ( Airbus , Rolls-Royce , DLR , Neste , NRC Canada , Manchester U. ) flew an Airbus A350-941 ( first A350 ever built ) powered by two Rolls-Royce Trent XWB-84 engines , using 100 percent Neste HEFA-SPK SAF in both engines . A reference flight with conventional Jet A-1 was also flown under similar conditions . The A350 took off from Toulouse ( Blagnac ) and the DLR Falcon 20-E research jet from Oberpfaffenhofen ( Munich ) ; they rendezvoused over the Mediterranean and southern France . <p> The Falcon tracked the A350 by maintaining specific distance intervals ( as near as around 100 meters ) in order to collect samples from the exhaust gases . The engines of the A350 were thoroughly tested before the flight , and it was determined that there was " no engineering obstacle " to using the engines solely on SAF . Thus , the aircraft maintained proper thrust and turbine operating temperatures while flying with the fuel blend . It was even observed that the reduced density and increased energy per kilogram provided an improved efficiency compared to conventional jet @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ full set of emissions data . . . is to fly an aircraft in real conditions , " said Airbus 's Steven Le Moing . In ECLIF3 , this meant airborne instruments probing contrail formation in situ . The A350 was fueled with 100 percent HEFA-SPK ( nearly 0 percent aromatics ) supplied by Neste , while the chase Falcon was equipped with state-of-the-art probes . <p> Every second , the Falcon logged GPS coordinates , temperature , pressure , and humidity , allowing assessment of the Schmidt-Appleman contrail criterion conditions . In other words , each flight generated a time-stamped emission index ( particles per kilogram of fuel ) under specific engine operating conditions and atmospheric conditions . According to DLR documentation , the Falcon was capable of measuring the exhaust gases from a distance of about 100 meters behind the A350 aircraft . ( On the ground , engine test stands and gas chromatography were used to determine the chemical composition of the fuel . ) <p> Engineers value emission indices ( EI ) -- the number of particles ( ice or soot ) per kg of fuel @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ the team computed apparent ice emission indices ( ice crystals/kg ) and nvPM EI for both fuels . Then , to estimate climate impact , these EI values were plugged into DLR 's contrail and climate models ( a contrail plume model and a global climate model ) . <p> In summary , the raw data were used in simulating contrail formation and radiative forcing . ( The observations made from the 1 -- 2 min old contrails were used to model the CoCiP/LIM plume model , and from there , the ECHAM5-CCMod model run by DLR calculated the overall radiative forcing from the contrails in a traffic situation in 2018 . ) <h> Emissions and contrail reduction <p> The in-flight tests yielded a substantial decline in soot and ice with 100 percent SAF . More specifically , the number of ice particles per kilogram of fuel in contrails following the SAF-powered A350 aircraft was reduced by 56 percent in comparison with Jet A-1 . ( That is , the ice EI appeared to be about 56 percent lower ) . In turn , the nvPM concentration , or soot @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ is that the HEFA-based fuel contained virtually no aromatics and only 97 percent sulfur compared with Jet A ( 0.0007 percent against 0.0211 percent by mass ) . <p> Equally important , ECLIF3 proved that current-generation engines and fuel systems can handle 100 percent bio-jet safely and reliably . " The results show how crucial SAF is for decarbonising air transport , " said Airbus engineer Mark Bentall . NASA and other labs had shown 50 percent blends help , but this is the first in situ proof that full SAF use sharply cuts contrail-forming particles . <p> As Markus Fischer ( DLR ) explains : " The use of 100 percent SAF can help us significantly reduce the climate-warming effect of contrails , in addition to lowering the carbon footprint of flying -- a clear sign of the effectiveness of SAF towards climate-compatible aviation " . <p> Put simply , pure SAF in both engines does n't just save life-cycle CO2 -- it thins the contrail clouds . And because contrail cirrus have lifetimes of only hours , this gives an immediate climate benefit ( unlike CO2 , which @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ demonstrates a practical lever for cutting aviation 's short-term warming . Rolls-Royce 's Alan Newby notes that high-blend SAF was shown to reduce both CO2 and non-CO2 forcing , and that " our Trent XWB-84 engine can run on 100 percent SAF " , proving feasibility . <h> Engineering lessons and scaling SAF <p> For aerospace engineers , the message is clear -- new-age engines can already be operated entirely on SAF , and such operations bring non-CO2 emissions reductions as well . No modifications were needed except checks of the fuel feeding system , indicating that certification in the future to allow only SAF will be possible . However , a major problem remains in supply . <p> Currently , SAF use is minimal -- in 2024 in the EU , it comprised just 0.6 percent of jet fuel consumption ( approximately 193,000 tonnes ) , reducing CO2 emissions by about 714 kilotonnes . Traditional jet , on the other hand , accounted for about 99.4 percent of consumption and was significantly cheaper . According to official EASA sources , SAF production costs reach from ? 2,085 to ? @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ biofuels -- some 3-4 times higher than that for regular kerosene ( ? 734/t ) . The cost of renewable e-fuels is even higher ( 11 ? jet fuel ) . <p> The public policy is forcing growth : The EU ReFuelEU Aviation , for example , requires 2 percent SAF in 2025 , rising to 70 percent in 2050 ; however , there is an enormous gap between targets and reality . In a recent Nature study , researchers showed that while roughly 9.1 Mt per year of global SAF production capacity has been committed through 2024 , only around 24 percent of this capacity became active by schedule . Production is covering less than 0.3 percent of annual global jet fuel demand . <p> However , the on-board findings of the ECLIF3 provide a quantifiable goal for engineers to work towards . As Neste 's Alexander Kueper observes , the data " provide scientific support to use SAF at higher concentrations than the currently approved 50 percent " . Coupled with the historical advantages ( an estimated 80 percent reduction in lifecycle carbon dioxide emissions for HEFA compared @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ SAF to drastically reduce the impact of air travel , both in the long term and short term . <p> As DLR 's Fischer concludes , this experiment is " a clear sign of the effectiveness of SAF towards climate-compatible aviation " . <h> Recommended Articles <p> Srishti started out as an editor for academic journal articles before switching to reportage . With a keen interest in all things science , Srishti is particularly drawn to beats covering medicine , sustainable architecture , gene studies , and bioengineering . When she is n't elbows-deep in research for her next feature , Srishti enjoys reading contemporary fiction and chasing after her cats .

It does not necessarily reflect the view of The Herald . <p> On this day in 2025 , a satellite built in Stevenage successfully launched into space , marking a significant advancement in environmental monitoring . <p> The forest-monitoring satellite , named Biomass , was developed , built , and tested by Airbus in Stevenage , alongside teams in Friedrichshafen , Germany , and Toulouse , France . <p> Biomass was designed to examine the world 's forests to better understand the carbon cycle and its impact on climate change . <p> During its five-year mission , the satellite would utilise a revolutionary radar instrument to scan the Earth 's forests from an altitude of 666km . <p> This radar was expected to deliver exceptionally accurate maps of tropical , temperate , and boreal forest @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ play in regulating the climate . <p> The spacecraft featured a 12-metre deployable antenna , which was crucial for capturing data that would illustrate changes in biomass due to forest loss and regrowth . <p> In addition to monitoring forests , Biomass was capable of measuring paleo aquifers in desert regions to identify new water sources in arid areas . <p> It would also contribute to observations of ice sheet dynamics , subsurface geology , and forest topography . <p> The radar was designed to penetrate the forest canopy , providing a completely new view of the Earth 's surface . <p> At the time , Alain Faur ? , head of space systems at Airbus Defence and Space , said : " Biomass will give scientists and climatologists unprecedented data on the state of the world 's forests , further enhancing the understanding of the climate cycle . <p> " The spacecraft is now safely in orbit and ready to deliver its precious data . " <p> The data captured by Biomass would support a UN climate change initiative aimed at reducing emissions due to deforestation . <h> Follow @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ adhere to the Independent Press Standards Organisation 's Editors ' Code of Practice . @@136761253 <h> The Irish Independent 's View : Urgent action is needed after yet another dire warning on climate <p> The Greenland ice sheet is melting at a frightening rate . Stock image : Getty <p> Editorial <p> Today at 06:30 <p> Another abrupt reminder that facts do not cease to exist because they are ignored has landed concerning attitudes to clim ? ate change . The latest " European State of the Climate " report has found Europe is now the fastest-warming continent . <p> The climate-change debate struggles to penetrate the thick wall of complacency that has been built around the subject , but one fact should stop us in our tracks -- for three consecutive weeks , temperatures inside the Arctic Circle breached 30C . <p> If we need further convincing , consider this : the Greenland ice sheet lost 139 billion tonnes of ice . That is equivalent to one-and-a-half times all the ice contained in the glaciers of the European Alps . <p> The consequences are immediate and obvious . Floods , storms , droughts and wildfires are threatening in a way never seen before . @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ at a rate of 0.56C a decade throughout the past 30 years . This is more than twice as fast as the global average , which is 0.27C a decade . <p> Meanwhile , 70pc of European rivers had below- ? average annual flows . <p> The evidence is irrefutable that change is happening at an accelerated pace . Unfortunately , our responses are not . <p> At the beginning of this month , we got another stark warning from our own Climate Change Advisory Council . Its report also highlighted how Ireland is in the midst of the climate crisis and we are not doing nearly enough to prevent it getting worse . <p> The record-breaking winds of 2025 's Storm ? owyn were a wake-up call in themselves . It proved to be the most costly storm in Irish insurance history . <p> This study leaves little doubt we must confront a choice between the disastrous and the unpalatable <p> In purely practical terms , the advisory council recommended that our early-warning systems and flood defences be improved . It also stressed the urgency of its previous recommendations @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ adaptation " . <p> However , politicians tend to baulk at enforcing measures that may be painful in the short-term , but hugely beneficial down the line . <p> The recognition that collectively we have an obligation to change the way we live for our children 's sake is the imperative of our age . <p> This study leaves little doubt that we must confront a choice between the disastrous and the unpalatable . <p> We saw with the recent fuel blockades how easy it is to set aside critical climate-change measures when faced with a public backlash . <p> Governments have to buy in to building resilience in adapting to these urgent changes . <p> Nature is screaming at us that we are on borrowed time . We see it in the storms and droughts and feel it in the rising -- or falling -- temperatures . <p> Debate about whether they are natural , man-made or somewhere in between is futile . Our only course is to respond . We are not the owners of this planet , but its keepers. 
@@136761594 <p> Ireland has been announced as co-host for next year 's sequel to the world 's first Conference on Transitioning Away from Fossil Fuels . <p> The announcement came at the conclusion of the inaugural conference held in Santa Marta , Colombia , over the past week . <p> Ireland will co-host with Tuvalu , one of the collections of low-lying small islands in the South Pacific at risk of disappearing as sea levels rise . <p> The venue for the conference has not yet been confirmed but Tuvalu has said it wants to host the physical gathering , despite having a population of just 11,000 and limited infrastructure for an international event . <p> The conference ended with agreement by the 60 countries attending to draw up national roadmaps setting how they will each wean their own societies and economies off oil , gas and coal . <p> The initiative grew from frustration with the annual United Nations climate summits or Cops , where all agreements must be unanimous , and the main fossil fuel producing and fossil fuel dependent countries repeatedly block progress . <p> Climate justice campaigners @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ step in ending fossil fuels . <p> ActionAid Ireland said the Government must not let the momentum slide and should begin work on a national plan to phase out fossil fuels straight away . <p> About 83 per cent of Ireland 's energy -- for transport , heating and electricity generation -- still comes from oil and gas . <p> " The conference was a watershed moment in which the collective mind became truly focused on the common cause of ending the fossil fuel era , " saidActionAid Ireland chief executive Karol Balfe . <p> Ireland was one of the original group of 24 countries to sign up to the initiative to have a " transition away " movement in parallel with the annual Cops . <p> Successive governments have also supported IrishAid to work closely with the Alliance of Small Island States ( Aosis ) in advocating for climate finance and assistance for small nations most at risk from climate change .

@@136778664 <h> Can you develop hay fever later in life ? Here 's what we know <p> Short answer : Yes , you can develop hay fever ( allergic rhinitis ) later in life , even if you 've never had allergies before . <p> While many people associate hay fever with childhood or early adulthood , the immune system is n't static , and sensitivities can emerge at almost any age . <p> At its core , hay fever is an immune overreaction to harmless substances like pollen , dust mites , or pet dander . Your body mistakenly identifies these as threats and releases histamine , causing symptoms like sneezing , itchy eyes , and congestion . <p> So why would this suddenly start happening after years of being symptom-free ? <h> 1 . Changes in the immune system <p> The immune system evolves over time . <p> As you age , it can become either less tolerant or more reactive to certain environmental triggers . A substance that your body previously ignored might begin to provoke a response . <h> 2 . Increased or new exposure <p> Moving @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ or even changing hobbies can expose you to new allergens . <p> For example , relocating from a city to a rural area might increase exposure to grass or tree pollen . <h> 3 . Cumulative exposure <p> Sometimes it 's not a sudden change but a gradual buildup . <p> Repeated exposure to an allergen over years can eventually " sensitise " your immune system , tipping it into an allergic response . <h> 4 . Hormonal shifts <p> Hormonal changes -- such as those occurring during pregnancy , menopause , or certain health conditions -- can influence immune responses and make allergies more likely to appear or worsen . <h> 5 . Environmental factors <p> Air pollution , climate change , and longer pollen seasons are increasing the overall allergen load in many regions . <p> Even if your sensitivity has n't changed much , the environment around you might have . <h> What symptoms look like in adults <p> Late-onset hay fever presents the same way as earlier-onset cases . Common symptoms include : <p> Frequent sneezing <p> Runny or blocked nose <p> Itchy eyes , nose @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ during high pollen seasons ) <p> Because these symptoms overlap with colds or sinus issues , adults sometimes misattribute them at first . <h> Can it go away again ? <p> It can -- but not always . Allergies may : <p> Stay consistent year to year <p> Worsen with continued exposure <p> Improve if exposure decreases or with treatment <p> In some cases , people also " outgrow " allergies , though this is less predictable in adulthood than in childhood . <h> What to do if you suspect new hay fever <p> If you 're noticing recurring seasonal symptoms : <p> Track timing : Do symptoms appear at the same time each year ? Check triggers : Are they worse outdoors or in specific environments ? Seek testing : A GP or allergist can confirm triggers through skin or blood tests
